## [2026-06-11 interactive] policy | Brian rule: never negotiate price with sponsors — sell ROI against their goals | Kerri

Brian, in interactive chat re: the NTOP email (which he approved, so no incident): "we never want to get into a one-to-one discussion with customers on price, because the price we sell sponsorship for is simply downstream of the value we're creating for them. Always lean into how we're delivering ROI against their goals, as opposed to making it a pricing conversation, because we're always going to lose that." Follow-up refinement same session: when a sponsor doesn't see the value, there are exactly two moves — (1) prove the value with evidence (audience data, past results, live examples), or (2) suggest a different product that delivers the value they're looking for. Codified as a "Price objections" section in `brain/wiki/workflows/hwfyi-package-quote-playbook.md`: never defend/itemize price or preemptively discount; redirect to ROI vs the sponsor's stated goals; the two value-objection moves; reshaping the package to fit a budget is allowed (changes value delivered, not haggling). Memory pointer at `~/.claude/.../memory/feedback_no_price_negotiation.md`. Applies to all sponsor drafts: price pushback replies, post-quote follow-ups, renewals.

## [2026-06-03 21:55 ET] fix | inbox-sweep-lock-liveness-bug | Kerri (Claude interactive)
Unblocked the stalled cross-runner sync flagged by the 21:51 gap-sweep — but the real cause was NOT just "Brian's WIP legitimately failing tests." The in-flight `inbox-sweep-lock.mjs` change added a holder-liveness check (same host + dead recorded PID → reclaim lock immediately, faster than the 90-min TTL). That check is unsound for THIS lock: it's a persistent cross-invocation lock (`acquire` writes the lock and exits; the sweep runs in a separate process; `release` removes it later), so the recorded `pid` is the ephemeral acquire process, always dead before any work begins → every same-host lock became instantly reclaimable → mutual exclusion destroyed (the exact overlapping-sweep pileup the lock exists to prevent per automations.md L30). `test/inbox-sweep-lock.test.js` caught it (second acquire returned acquired:true/exit 0 instead of busy/exit 2). FIX: removed the liveness check, restored TTL-only staleness (the 90-min crash fuse is the only valid backstop here). `npm test` now 65/65 green; `npm run check` clean. This means the gap-sweep's recommended fix — making the sync gate SELECTIVE so markdown bypasses failing code — is unnecessary and undesirable: the all-or-nothing red-build gate is correct and should stay; we fixed the actual bug instead. On next sync (Stop hook or `bash scripts/kerri-sync.sh`) tests pass → all of today's stranded writes (NOW.md baton, 4 meeting notes, company/routing updates, eod SKILL) commit + push to main and the conflict marker auto-clears (kerri-sync.sh L94). FOLLOW-UP for Brian: the *intent* (recover faster than 90 min from a crashed sweep) is valid but needs a different design — e.g. the long-running worker writes its own PID, or an flock-style held-open FD — not the acquire process's PID. Did not re-attempt that here.

## [2026-06-02 00:47 ET] fix | et-date-skew-in-routines | Kerri (Claude interactive)
Fixed the +1-day ET mis-dating flagged in the 2026-06-01 21:55 gap-sweep (task `YzhMZ2ZuOTdkNmlndWxLaQ`, class K). Root cause confirmed: routines stamped ET-labeled writes from the harness `currentDate`, which is **UTC** — so in the 8pm–midnight ET window it's already tomorrow. Two-layer fix (prompt-only, no send/gate/identity/finance change → routine class): (1) canonical **"Date & time handling"** rule added to `agent-prompts/CLAUDE-ROUTINES.md`; (2) load-bearing date-stamp guard added to the three in-window routines the shims read directly — `kerri-inbox-sweep` (owns the NOW.md baton, runs to ~10:45pm ET), `kerri-gap-sweep` (21:41 ET; also fixes its class-K NOW.md-vs-log staleness compare), `kerri-brain-push` (22:00 ET). Live for tonight's runs (shims read local agent-prompts/). Did NOT hand-edit the live NOW.md baton date (the */15 sweep owns it) — only added an in-flight note. Routines stamping dates outside the window (morning-brief already uses America/New_York; eod 18:31, cold-outreach/lead-research daytime) are covered by the canonical rule.

- 2026-06-10: inbox-sweep (claude) caught + fixed a STEP 5 cursor-corruption write (2nd confirmed occurrence of the bug tracked by open suggestion MDZ3WmczQncteTRqenhKUQ). The state/grade node write read `process.env.NOW_ISO` but the shell var was set without `export`, so all 4 mailbox `lastSuccessfulSweepAt` + the grade `at` were written as undefined/blank. Gmail seenMessageIds (incl. new Mercury+Doug ids) survived the bad write. Otherwise a quiet run: no task/send/skip/redo, no Brian-facing alert. Recurrence strengthens the case for routing state writes through a validating helper per the open suggestion.
